Full answer
-----------

The documents set these rules:

Interim quarantine rule (Doc. 4, published May 27, 2026)

- Deadline: June 26, 2026
- Acceptable methods: only the Federal eRulemaking Portal (no mail address is listed; email is prohibited)
- Received June 25: 30 letters + 18 portal comments
- 30 letters → wrong method
- 18 portal → correct method and on time ✓

June 21 Ebola order (Doc. 5)

- Deadline: July 10, 2026
- Acceptable methods: portal or mail
- Received June 25: 21 letters → correct method and on time ✓

Subsequent portal batches (each arrived 20 days after the prior order’s comment period closed)

- June order (prior: original order closed June 22) → arrived ~July 12; June deadline was July 10 → late (22 fail timing)
- July order (prior: June order closed July 10) → arrived ~July 30; July deadline was July 31 → on time ✓ (19)
- August order (prior: July order closed July 31) → arrived ~August 20; August deadline was Sept. 1 → on time ✓ (12)

So the ones meeting both method and timing are: 18 (rule, portal) + 21 (June order, letters) + 19 (July order, portal) + 12 (August order, portal) = 70

Full answer
-----------

The counts depend on each notice’s submission method rules and comment deadlines.

Interim quarantine rule (Document 4)

- Allowed methods: Federal eRulemaking Portal or mail; email is not accepted.
- Deadline: June 26, 2026 (must be received by then).
- Received: 30 letters (mail) + 18 portal comments on June 25, 2026.
- All 48 arrived before the deadline via permitted methods.

→ 48 meet requirements.

Ebola entry orders (Documents 5, 6, 7) All are portal submissions (correct method), but they arrived 19 days after issuance:

- June order (issued June 21): 19 days later = July 10, 2026.

Deadline: July 10, 2026 (“on or before”). Received: 22 → 22 meet timing.

- July order (issued July 13): 19 days later = August 1, 2026.

Deadline: July 31, 2026. Received: 21 on Aug 1 → 0 meet timing.

- August order (issued Aug 12): 19 days later = August 31, 2026.

Deadline: September 1, 2026 (“on or before”). Received: 12 → 12 meet timing.

Total that met both method and timing: 48 + 22 + 0 + 12 = 82.

Full answer

To determine how many comments met CDC's requirements on method and timing, I need to analyze the submission requirements and deadlines for each rule/order as specified in the Federal Register documents, then evaluate the comments described in the question.

1. Interim Quarantine Rule (Document 4, published May 27, 2026):

- Deadline: "Comments must be received by June 26, 2026"
- Required method: "Because of staff and resource limitations, all comments must be submitted electronically to www.regulations.gov." (Mail/letters explicitly not accepted)
- Docket: CDC-2026-0991

Comments received:

- June 25: 30 letters (mail) + 18 portal comments (electronic via regulations.gov)
- July 10: 22 portal comments

Evaluation:

- 30 letters (June 25): Invalid method (mail not accepted for this rule)
- 18 portal comments (June 25): Valid (electronic method, received before June 26 deadline)
- 22 portal comments (July 10): Invalid timing (received after June 26 deadline)

Valid for interim rule: 18 comments

2. June 21 Ebola Entry Order (Document 5, issued June 21, 2026):

- Deadline: "Written comments must be received on or before July 10, 2026"
- Required methods: "Federal eRulemaking Portal: http://www.regulations.gov" OR "Mail: Division of Global Migration Health..." (Email explicitly prohibited)
- Docket: CDC-2026-0892

Comments received:

- June 25: 21 letters (mail)
- July 10: 22 portal comments

Evaluation:

- 21 letters (June 25): Valid (mail is accepted method, received before July 10 deadline)
- 22 portal comments (July 10): Valid (electronic method accepted, "on or before July 10" includes July 10)

Valid for Ebola order: 21 + 22 = 43 comments

Total comments meeting both method and timing requirements: 18 + 43 = 61

I need to check the documents to see what CDC's requirements were for submitting comments (method and timing), and then determine how many of the mentioned comments met those requirements.

First, let's look at Document 4, which is the interim final rule (IFR) about Control of Communicable Diseases; Foreign Quarantine.

From Document 4:
"DATES: Effective date: This interim final rule is effective on May 22, 2026. Comment date: Comments must be received by June 26, 2026."

"ADDRESSES: You may submit comments, identified by CDC-2026-0991, by the following method:
Federal eRulemaking Portal: http://www.regulations.gov. Follow the instructions for submitting comments.
Instructions: All submissions received must include the agency name and docket number or Regulatory Information Number (RIN) for this rulemaking. All comments received will be posted without change to http://regulations.gov, including any personal information provided. For access to the docket to read background documents or comments received, go to http://www.regulations.gov.
Any comment that is submitted will be shared with the Department of Homeland Security and the Department of State and will also be made available to the public. Comments must be identified by CDC-2026-0991. Because of staff and resource limitations, all comments must be submitted electronically to www.regulations.gov. Follow the ``Submit a comment'' instructions.
Warning: Do not include any personally identifiable information (such as name, address, or other contact information) or confidential business information that you do not want publicly disclosed. All comments may be posted on the internet and can be retrieved by most internet search engines. No deletions, modifications, or redactions will be made to comments received."

So for the interim quarantine rule (Document 4), CDC required:
- Comments must be received by June 26, 2026
- Comments must be submitted electronically to www.regulations.gov
- Comments must be identified by CDC-2026-0991

All relevant comments received will be posted without change to http://regulations.gov, including any personal information provided. So for the June 21 Ebola entry order (Document 5), CDC required:
